## DETAILED RESEARCH REPORT: Triton tl.dot_scaled M=64, K=1536 Failure

Based on comprehensive research of the skill files, submission code, and test specifications, here is my analysis of the tl.dot_scaled bug and alternative approaches.

### CONTEXT SUMMARY

**Test Shapes (from task.yml):**
- m=8, n=2112, k=7168 ✓ Passes
- m=16, n=3072, k=1536 ✓ Passes
- **m=64, n=3072, k=1536 ✗ FAILS (large element-wise errors)**
- m=256, n=2880, k=512 ✓ Passes

**Current Kernel:** `submission_triton_v4.py` uses:
- BLOCK_M=32 (fixed for all shapes after v3 failed with BLOCK_M=64)
- BLOCK_N=64
- BLOCK_K=64 (minimum for gfx950 tl.dot_scaled)
- e8m0_unshuffle to recover B_scale from B_scale_sh (saves 15µs vs re-quantizing)

**Current Performance:** ~24.3µs geomean (ranked via aiter.gemm_a4w4)

---

## HYPOTHESIS ANALYSIS (Ranked by Likelihood)

### HYPOTHESIS 1: A_scale Padding Mismatch at M=64 (HIGHEST LIKELIHOOD - 70%)

**Root Cause:**
`dynamic_mxfp4_quant(A)` returns A_scale with **padded dimensions**. The padding varies by M:

```
For M=64, K=1536:
  K_half = 1536/2 = 768 (packed bytes)
  scale_K = K_half // 16 = 48 (scales per row, since each scale covers 32 fp4 = 16 bytes)
  
  dynamic_mxfp4_quant returns A_scale shaped:
    [padded_M, padded_scale_K]  where padded_M >= 64, padded_scale_K may be >= 48
```

**Evidence:**
1. Line 109 of research_strategy.md explicitly states: "Hypothesis: A_scale padding from dynamic_mxfp4_quant mismatch at M=64"
2. The kernel **assumes** `A_scale` has shape `[M, SCALE_PER_BLOCK]`, but `dynamic_mxfp4_quant` may return `[padded_M, ...]`
3. When loading `a_scale` at line 84-87 of submission_triton_v4.py:
   ```python
   a_scale = tl.load(
       A_scale_ptr + offs_m[:, None] * stride_as_m + offs_scale_k[None, :] * stride_as_k,
       mask=mask_m[:, None], other=0,
   )
   ```
   If `stride_as_m` is computed from a padded M dimension, then strides will be wrong when actual M < padded_M.

4. **Why only M=64 fails:** For m=8, 16, 256, either:
   - Padding doesn't occur (power of 2), OR
   - Padding happens but stride mismatch is hidden by small element count OR
   - BLOCK_M=32 happens to align with padding boundaries for other shapes

**Fix Path:**
```python
# After quantization:
A_fp4, A_scale_padded = dynamic_mxfp4_quant(A)
# Slice to exact shape BEFORE passing to kernel
A_scale = A_scale_padded[:M, :scale_K].contiguous()  # [M, scale_K] not padded
A_u8 = A_fp4.view(torch.uint8)
A_scale_u8 = A_scale.view(torch.uint8)  # Now strides are accurate
```

**Why this matters for tl.dot_scaled:**
The hardware `tl.dot_scaled` operation is sensitive to tensor metadata. If stride(0) of `a_scale` assumes padded_M=128 but actual M=64, the indexing formula `offs_m[:, None] * stride_as_m` loads from wrong memory locations, producing garbage values in the scale tensor.

---

### HYPOTHESIS 2: K_HALF Integer Division / Scale Alignment Issue (MEDIUM LIKELIHOOD - 45%)

**Root Cause:**
For m=64, k=1536:
- K_half = 1536 / 2 = 768 (packed uint8 bytes)
- SCALE_PER_BLOCK = BLOCK_K // 16 = 64 // 16 = 4
- Number of K-tiles = ceil(K_half / BLOCK_K) = ceil(768 / 64) = 12 tiles

However, the kernel uses:
```python
for k_start in range(0, K_HALF, BLOCK_K):  # [0, 64, 128, ..., 768]
    scale_k_start = k_start // 16
    offs_scale_k = scale_k_start + tl.arange(0, SCALE_PER_BLOCK)
```

**Issue:** At the final K-tile iteration (k_start=704):
- scale_k_start = 704 // 16 = 44
- offs_scale_k = [44, 45, 46, 47] — but scale_K might be 48 (so indices 44-47 are valid)

But for edge cases where K_half is oddly padded by dynamic_mxfp4_quant, scale indices could exceed the scale tensor dimension.

**Evidence:**
- Line 81-82: `scale_k_start = k_start // 16` uses integer division
- Line 123 in skill shows: `SCALE_PER_BLOCK: tl.constexpr = BLOCK_K // 16`
- For BLOCK_K=64, SCALE_PER_BLOCK=4, which means we load 4 scale entries per 64-byte tile
- But K_half=768 might not align perfectly to scale group boundaries

**Why only M=64 fails:**
Smaller M shapes (8,16) have fewer K-tiles or smaller K overall. M=256 with K=512 has K_half=256 (4 tiles of 64 bytes each), which aligns perfectly. M=64, K=1536 produces exactly 12 tiles with edge alignment issues.

---

### HYPOTHESIS 3: BLOCK_M=32 Too Small for tl.dot_scaled with M=64 Batch (LOW LIKELIHOOD - 25%)

**Root Cause:**
The skill file `amd-gfx950-tl-dot-scaled-constraints.md` states:
> BLOCK_M minimum: 16

But it also warns:
> **Symptom if violated:** 3/4 test shapes pass, smallest shape (m=8) fails with ~6% error.

**Reverse issue:** What if BLOCK_M is *too small* for certain M values?

For m=64, using BLOCK_M=32 creates 2 tiles in M dimension. Each tile processes 32 rows.
The tl.dot_scaled operation might have undocumented requirements on how many rows are processed *within a single program* (not per tile).

**Evidence:**
- submission_triton_v4.py line 125-127: "BLOCK_M=64 failed for m=64 — need to investigate why"
- They reverted to BLOCK_M=32 "for all shapes (proven correct for m=8,16,256)" but never tested if 32 is actually correct for m=64
- The issue might be that BLOCK_M=32 with m=64 creates some interaction with hardware tile scheduling that breaks for that specific shape

**Why only M=64 fails:**
- m=8: 1 tile of BLOCK_M=32 (partial, mask handles < 32)
- m=16: 1 tile of BLOCK_M=32 (partial)
- m=64: 2 complete tiles of BLOCK_M=32
- m=256: 8 complete tiles of BLOCK_M=32

The 2-tile case might trigger a specific HW bug.

---

### HYPOTHESIS 4: B_scale Layout Transpose Error (MEDIUM-LOW LIKELIHOOD - 35%)

**Root Cause:**
The skill warns (line 72-84):
```
RHS scale: [BLOCK_N, SCALE_PER_BLOCK] (N-first, NOT transposed)
Even though B is stored transposed as [K//2, N], the RHS scale must be **N-first**
```

In submission_triton_v4.py:
```python
B_t, B_scale_raw = _prepare_b(B_q, B_scale_sh, N, K)
# B_scale_raw is recovered via _e8m0_unshuffle
```

The `_e8m0_unshuffle` function (lines 13-18) attempts to reverse the shuffle:
```python
scale = scale_shuffled.view(sm // 32, sn // 8, 4, 16, 2, 2)
scale = scale.permute(0, 5, 3, 1, 4, 2).contiguous()
scale = scale.view(sm, sn)
return scale[:orig_m, :orig_n]
```

**Issue:** This permutation sequence might be **wrong for the specific (M, N, K) tuple** of m=64, n=3072, k=1536.

The unshuffle assumes the shuffled layout was created with specific tile dimensions. But the seed/random state in the test might produce shuffled B_scale with different internal packing.

**Why only M=64 fails:**
- Other shapes happen to have N values (2112, 2880, 4096) that align with 8-element chunking assumptions
- m=64, n=3072: n=3072 = 3072 / 8 = 384 chunks. If shuffle expected 256 chunks or 512 chunks, permutation indices are off.

---

### HYPOTHESIS 5: Software Correctness of `tl.dot_scaled` Instruction (VERY LOW LIKELIHOOD - 10%)

**Root Cause:**
The AMD MI355X (gfx950) `tl.dot_scaled` operation for e2m1 (MXFP4) is relatively new. There might be a **hardware bug** or **undefined behavior** triggered by the specific sequence of:
- BLOCK_M=64 or 32 (affecting register allocation)
- BLOCK_K=64 (minimum allowed)
- BLOCK_N=64
- m=64 (matching power-of-2 tile blocking)

**Evidence:**
- The skill file only says "Discovered during Luma AMD Speedrun competition (March 2026)"
- No public bug tracker mentions show up for `tl.dot_scaled` + "e2m1" + gfx950
- This is bleeding-edge hardware (MI355X released 2025)

**Why this is unlikely:**
If it were a hardware bug, we'd expect random data corruption, NaN values, or GPU hangs. Instead, the errors are "large element-wise errors" — systematic, not random. This points to software logic, not HW.

---

## ALTERNATIVE APPROACHES (Beyond tl.dot_scaled)

### PATH 1: Direct aiter.gemm_a4w4 (Already Proven - 24.3µs)

**Status:** Unexplored
**Requirements:**
- All tensors must be `torch.uint8` views
- B layout is [N, K//2] row-major

**Pros:**

- Used in Helion/Origami optimizations

**Cons:**

### PATH 3: Inline BF16 → FP4 Quantization (Skip dynamic_mxfp4_quant)
**Status:** Partially explored (triton-fp4-inline-quantization skill)
**Idea:** Fuse quantization into the GEMM kernel to eliminate 16µs overhead

**Challenges (from skill):**
- 3D tensor integer indexing not supported in Triton JIT
- E8M0 scale formula must normalize to fp4_max=6.0, not 2.0
- BLOCK_K must be >= 128 BF16 elements (not 64) for tl.dot_scaled inside fused kernel

**Evidence:**
- Previous attempt in research_strategy.md (line 172): "Triton bf16 fused quant+GEMM: CORRECTNESS FAIL"

**Why this failed before:**
- torch.mm(A, B_bf16.T) ≠ MXFP4-quantized reference (16k+ mismatches)
- The quantization formula was wrong or scale expansion was wrong

### PATH 4: Use Pre-Shuffled B_scale (Skip e8m0_unshuffle)
**Status:** Attempted in submission_voptimized.py
**Idea:** Avoid unshuffle overhead by using pre-shuffled B_scale directly

**Code (voptimized.py):**
```python
A_q = A_q_raw.view(dtypes.fp4x2)
A_scale_shuffled = e8m0_shuffle(A_scale_raw).view(dtypes.fp8_e8m0)
C = aiter.gemm_a4w4(A_q, B_shuffle, A_scale_shuffled, B_scale_sh,
                    dtype=dtypes.bf16, bpreshuffle=True)
```

**Result:** This is the current best (24.3µs). No improvement over e8m0_unshuffle path.

     1→"""
     2→MLA (Multi-head Latent Attention) decode kernel — submission template.
     3→
     4→Implement custom_kernel() to beat the aiter a8w8 reference (fp8 Q + fp8 KV).
     5→
     6→DeepSeek R1 forward_absorb MLA config:
     7→  total_num_heads  = 128    (query heads before TP split)
     8→  num_heads        = 128 // tp  (query heads per device, tp=4 → 32, tp=8 → 16)
     9→  num_kv_heads     = 1      (shared latent KV head)
    10→  kv_lora_rank     = 512    (latent dim)
    11→  qk_rope_head_dim = 64     (RoPE dim)
    12→  qk_head_dim      = 576    (kv_lora_rank + qk_rope_head_dim, absorbed q/k dim)
    13→  v_head_dim       = 512    (= kv_lora_rank, output dim)
    14→  sm_scale         = 1/sqrt(576)
    15→
    16→KV buffer format (forward_absorb):
    17→  - Full 576 dims used as keys (for Q@K^T score computation)
    18→  - First 512 dims (kv_lora_rank) used as values (for output computation)
    19→
    20→Input tuple:
    21→  q:          (total_q, num_heads, 576) bfloat16 — absorbed query
    22→  kv_data:    dict with three KV cache formats:
    23→    kv_data["bf16"]  — Tensor (total_kv, 1, 576) bfloat16
    24→    kv_data["fp8"]   — (Tensor, Tensor): kv_buffer fp8 (total_kv,1,576) + scalar scale
    25→    kv_data["mxfp4"] — (Tensor, Tensor): kv_buffer fp4x2 (total_kv,1,288) + fp8_e8m0 scale
    26→  qo_indptr:  (batch_size + 1,)        int32    — query segment pointers
    27→  kv_indptr:  (batch_size + 1,)        int32    — KV segment pointers
    28→  config:     dict with MLA parameters (includes num_heads computed from tp)
    29→
    30→Output:
    31→  attention output: (total_q, num_heads, 512) bfloat16
    32→
    33→The reference uses aiter's a8w8 persistent MLA kernel (fp8 Q + fp8 KV),
    34→which is ~2-3x faster than bf16. To beat it, consider:
    35→  1. Use mxfp4 KV cache for even lower memory bandwidth
    36→     - Fuse dequantization with attention to avoid bf16 materialization
    37→  2. Custom kernel with tighter memory access patterns
    38→  3. MQA: 1 KV head shared across num_heads query heads — minimize redundant memory loads
    39→  4. Variable-length batching: indptr-based segmented attention
    40→  5. Split K/V from buffer: full 576 dims for keys, first 512 dims for values
    41→"""
    42→
    43→import torch
    44→import torch.nn.functional as F
    45→from aiter import dtypes as aiter_dtypes
    46→from task import input_t, output_t
    47→
    48→
    49→FP8_DTYPE = aiter_dtypes.fp8
    50→
    51→# QKV dtype for custom_kernel dispatch: "bf16", "fp8", or "mxfp4"
    52→QKV_DTYPE = "fp8"
    53→
    54→
    55→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    56→# Dispatcher: select kernel based on QKV_DTYPE
    57→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    58→
    59→
    60→def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
    61→    """Dispatch to the appropriate kernel based on QKV_DTYPE."""
    62→    if QKV_DTYPE == "fp8":
    63→        return custom_kernel_fp8(data)
    64→    elif QKV_DTYPE == "bf16":
    65→        return custom_kernel_bf16(data)
    66→    else:
    67→        raise ValueError(f"Invalid QKV_DTYPE: {QKV_DTYPE}")
    68→
    69→
    70→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    71→# FP8 quantization helper (per-tensor, sglang style)
    72→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    73→
    74→
    75→def quantize_fp8(tensor: torch.Tensor) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
    76→    """Dynamic per-tensor FP8 quantization. Returns (fp8_tensor, scale)."""
    77→    finfo = torch.finfo(FP8_DTYPE)
    78→    amax = tensor.abs().amax().clamp(min=1e-12)
    79→    scale = amax / finfo.max
    80→    fp8_tensor = (tensor / scale).clamp(min=finfo.min, max=finfo.max).to(FP8_DTYPE)
    81→    return fp8_tensor, scale.to(torch.float32).reshape(1)
    82→
    83→
    84→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    85→# Baseline: bf16 Q + bf16 KV — naive torch attention
    86→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    87→
    88→
    89→def custom_kernel_bf16(data: input_t) -> output_t:
    90→    q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config = data
    91→
    92→    num_heads = config["num_heads"]
    93→    kv_lora_rank = config["kv_lora_rank"]
    94→    sm_scale = config["sm_scale"]
    95→
    96→    # This naive baseline uses bf16 KV directly.
    97→    # For better performance, use kv_data["fp8"] or kv_data["mxfp4"]
    98→    # which are (kv_buffer, kv_scale) tuples. See docstring for optimization opportunities.
    99→    kv_buffer_bf16 = kv_data["bf16"]
   100→
   101→    batch_size = qo_indptr.shape[0] - 1
   102→    out_list = []
   103→
   104→    for i in range(batch_size):
   105→        q_s, q_e = int(qo_indptr[i].item()), int(qo_indptr[i + 1].item())
   106→        kv_s, kv_e = int(kv_indptr[i].item()), int(kv_indptr[i + 1].item())
   107→
   108→        qi = q[q_s:q_e]  # (seq_q, nhead, 576)
   109→        kvc = kv_buffer_bf16[kv_s:kv_e, 0]  # (seq_kv, 576)  squeeze kv_heads dim
   110→
   111→        # Key: full 576 dims; Value: first 512 dims (kv_lora_rank)
   112→        ki = kvc  # (seq_kv, 576) — broadcast over heads
   113→        vi = kvc[:, :kv_lora_rank]  # (seq_kv, 512)
   114→
   115→        # Attention: (nhead, seq_q, 576) @ (576, seq_kv) → (nhead, seq_q, seq_kv)
   116→        qi_t = qi.float().permute(1, 0, 2)  # (nhead, seq_q, 576)
   117→        scores = torch.matmul(qi_t * sm_scale, ki.float().T)  # (nhead, seq_q, seq_kv)
   118→
   119→        scores = F.softmax(scores, dim=-1)
   120→
   121→        # Output: (nhead, seq_q, seq_kv) @ (seq_kv, 512) → (nhead, seq_q, 512)
   122→        oi = torch.matmul(scores, vi.float())  # (nhead, seq_q, 512)
   123→        oi = oi.permute(1, 0, 2)  # (seq_q, nhead, 512)
   124→        out_list.append(oi.to(torch.bfloat16))
   125→
   126→    return torch.cat(out_list, dim=0)
   127→
   128→
   129→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   130→# FP8 Q + FP8 KV — torch._scaled_mm based attention
   131→#
   132→# Quantize Q to fp8, use fp8 KV from kv_data["fp8"].
   133→# QK^T and softmax@V both use torch._scaled_mm for fp8×fp8 matmul.
   134→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   135→
   136→
   137→def custom_kernel_fp8(data: input_t) -> output_t:
   138→    q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config = data
   139→
   140→    num_heads = config["num_heads"]
   141→    kv_lora_rank = config["kv_lora_rank"]
   142→    qk_head_dim = config["qk_head_dim"]
   143→    sm_scale = config["sm_scale"]
   144→
   145→    # FP8 KV buffer and scale
   146→    kv_buffer_fp8, kv_scale_fp8 = kv_data["fp8"]
   147→    kv_fp8_2d = kv_buffer_fp8.view(-1, qk_head_dim)  # (total_kv, 576) fp8
   148→
   149→    # Quantize Q to fp8
   150→    q_fp8, q_scale = quantize_fp8(q)  # q_fp8: (total_q, 16, 576) fp8
   151→
   152→    batch_size = qo_indptr.shape[0] - 1
   153→    out_list = []
   154→
   155→    scale_one = torch.ones(1, dtype=torch.float32, device="cuda")
   156→
   157→    for i in range(batch_size):
   158→        q_s, q_e = int(qo_indptr[i].item()), int(qo_indptr[i + 1].item())
   159→        kv_s, kv_e = int(kv_indptr[i].item()), int(kv_indptr[i + 1].item())
   160→        seq_q = q_e - q_s
   161→        seq_kv = kv_e - kv_s
   162→
   163→        # Q: (seq_q * nhead, 576) fp8,  K: (seq_kv, 576) fp8
   164→        qi_fp8 = q_fp8[q_s:q_e].reshape(seq_q * num_heads, qk_head_dim)  # (seq_q*16, 576)
   165→        ki_fp8 = kv_fp8_2d[kv_s:kv_e]  # (seq_kv, 576)
   166→
   167→        # QK^T via _scaled_mm: (seq_q*16, 576) @ (seq_kv, 576).T -> (seq_q*16, seq_kv)
   168→        # _scaled_mm expects (M,K) @ (N,K).T  where b is row-major contiguous
   169→        raw_scores = torch._scaled_mm(
   170→            qi_fp8,
   171→            ki_fp8.t(),
   172→            scale_a=q_scale,
   173→            scale_b=kv_scale_fp8,
   174→            out_dtype=torch.float32,
   175→        )
   176→        # raw_scores: (seq_q*16, seq_kv)
   177→        scores = raw_scores.view(seq_q, num_heads, seq_kv).permute(
   178→            1, 0, 2
   179→        )  # (nhead, seq_q, seq_kv)
   180→        scores = scores * sm_scale
   181→        scores = F.softmax(scores, dim=-1)
   182→
   183→        # V: first 512 dims of KV buffer (bf16 for softmax@V since scores are float)
   184→        kv_bf16 = kv_data["bf16"]
   185→        vi = kv_bf16[kv_s:kv_e, 0, :kv_lora_rank].float()  # (seq_kv, 512)
   186→
   187→        # softmax @ V: (nhead, seq_q, seq_kv) @ (seq_kv, 512) -> (nhead, seq_q, 512)
   188→        oi = torch.matmul(scores, vi)
   189→        oi = oi.permute(1, 0, 2)  # (seq_q, nhead, 512)
   190→        out_list.append(oi.to(torch.bfloat16))
   191→
   192→    return torch.cat(out_list, dim=0)
   193→

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Three breakthroughs identified:**

1. **MoE**: The `aiter-kernel-parameter-semantics` skill has a verified table showing KSPLIT=4 drops E=257/bs=16 from 152.7→91.3µs (**-40%**) and E=257/bs=128 from 239→172µs (**-28%**). We're currently using KSPLIT=0 for these shapes! This is free performance left on the table.

2. **MLA**: The current submission is a **naive Python loop** with `torch._scaled_mm`. It's NOT using the aiter ASM persistent kernel at all. The Phase 17 three-regime routing (einsum for small batches, ASM for medium/large) achieved 67.8µs vs our current 90µs. That's a 25% improvement just by restoring the right kernel.

3. **GEMM**: The tl.dot_scaled bug at M=64 may be due to the Group-M swizzle producing incorrect tile IDs when `num_pid_m=2` (specifically when the group wraps). A simpler 2D grid without swizzle could fix it.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`
